# Syndicate — Work Lanes

> Lanes are exclusive by file path. Two lanes may not claim the same file.
> Max concurrent OPEN lanes: 3 (see `state.md`).
> Managed by `/lane`. Do not hand-edit while a session is running.

> **History lives in `lanes_history.md`.** This file is read at the start of
> every session, so it carries each lane's CURRENT state plus one prior block --
> **plus any block that declares file claims**, which `lane-guard` reads from
> here and nowhere else. 36 superseded blocks (2,667 lines) were moved out
> verbatim on 2026-08-18. Nothing was summarised or deleted: if a lane's earlier
> reasoning matters, it is there under the same slug.

#### ORPHAN SWEEP 2026-08-18 ~21:4xZ — 8 lanes RELEASED, 32 claims dropped, contested-file invariant CLEARED

**Measured with `lane-guard.py`'s OWN `_claims()`**, not the simplified copy in
`check_lane_invariants.py` — the two disagree, and the difference decides
outcomes. The checker lacks the guard's `_is_disclaimer` / `_claimable_prefix`
handling, so it reported 70 claims / 12 OPEN lanes where the guard actually saw
**102 claims / 17 OPEN lanes**. Read the guard when the question is "is this

**THE 7 REMAINING `OPEN`-UNDER-`## Archived lanes` ARE NOT MINE TO FIX.** Every
one belongs to a live or uncertain lane above, and the remedy is to MOVE the
block above the `## Archived lanes` marker — which is editing another lane's
block. Left for each owner. The hazard is real but latent: their claims work
today and would be dropped silently by a future archive pass.

**Method note for the next sweep.** `.syndicate/.current-lane.<uuid>` marker
filenames match archived `sessionId`s exactly (6 of 13 did), so a marker whose
id resolves to an ARCHIVED session is hard evidence the lane is orphaned. The
markers for running sessions did NOT match any roster id, so the mapping proves
death, never life — do not invert it.

### mlb-roster-substrate-probe — OPEN — opened 2026-09-10 — session 2edf8b82-9f8a-4d32-bf26-ca43ecd1ea5a
- Goal: answer ONE question that cannot be answered from outside refresh-worker — **does `daily_pitcher_props/snapshots/<date>/roster_objs/roster_obj_*.json` resolve on that service, and how many of its dates are `>= 2026-07-20`** (clear of the HRR contamination boundary `#624` step 2 records). A boot-time PROBE that reports; it changes no model and writes no artifact.
- WHY IT IS A PROBE AND NOT THE RE-FIT: `#624` step 3's re-fit reads that path, the local checkout has only 13 dates and **every one is at or before the HRR boundary**, and production's export API cannot see the tree at all. Shipping a re-fit runner on an unverified path is the deployed-inert shape `#625`(3) exists to catch — and this session already hit that shape once tonight (the NFL join stamped 0 rows against real data while 13 unit tests passed).
- IT IS A CLASS, NOT ONE SCRIPT: **11 scripts read that path**, including `sim_input_checklist.py`, which `CLAUDE.md` names as the GATING input checklist every model engine must pass.
- Hypothesis: refresh-worker HAS the tree (it writes roster artifacts — `--write-roster-artifacts` defaults `on` and production does not override it) but possibly under `data/daily/snapshots/` rather than `daily_pitcher_props/snapshots/`, which is a DIFFERENT tree and the one the re-fit cannot reach via `SYNDICATE_MLB_DATA_ROOT` (the suffix is hard-coded).
- Falsification test: if the probe prints a count for BOTH trees and they disagree with this hypothesis, the hypothesis is wrong and the re-fit's path handling — not the data — is the defect.
- Verification: a `[refresh_worker] MLB_ROSTER_SUBSTRATE` line on refresh-worker after deploy, printed UNCONDITIONALLY including when both trees are absent, so an absent tree and a failed probe are distinguishable.
- Cost: one directory scan at BOOT ONLY. Not per-tick — `learnings.md`/`#241`: worker periodic work is never free.
- Files: NONE claimed. The only write is ADDITIVE (one boot-probe function plus one call line) into a file OPEN lane `ncaaf-live-resim-wire` holds — **declared in that lane's block rather than claimed here**, because two OPEN holders on one path is what `check_lane_invariants` refuses.

### nfl-prop-grading — OPEN — opened 2026-09-10 — session 2edf8b82-9f8a-4d32-bf26-ca43ecd1ea5a (archived 2026-09-11, so UNOWNED)
- Goal: make `bet_status_nfl` grade NFL player props from ESPN's per-player box instead of refusing every one as `nfl_props_not_gradeable_from_scoreboard`, so the 2026-09-09 user decision to leave NFL props stakeable can produce a production grade. — **GOAL: NOT MET.** `2259edf8` + `0ceb9636` removed the refusals (measured 2026-09-10 9:59 CT). First-grade reading, 2026-09-11 08:50 CT: NCAAF GRADED in production (20 FAMU @ MIA orders, each outcome consistent with the score). But SF @ LAR carried 0 orders: every NFL order is a player prop on a 09-13..09-15 game, so no NFL prop has graded yet. LEFT: the first NFL prop grade after Sunday 09-13 (scheduled task `nfl-prop-settled-grade`, 09-15). See `deploys.md` 2026-09-11 08:50 CT. (Verdict written by session 2edf8b82, which took scheduled task nfl-ncaaf-first-grade-reading's reading by hand because the task never dispatched.)
- Why: `findings_2026-09-10_nfl_opener_end_to_end.md`. The refusal was already firing on 1 order, and the scheduled grading task (`nfl-prop-settled-grade`, 09-15) would otherwise have to grade props itself.
- Method: extend the ONE existing ESPN parser (`nfl/live_player_box.py`) additively with count stats (receptions, rush/pass attempts, completions, interceptions thrown) instead of writing a third parser. `interceptions` is read from the `passing` group ONLY, because ESPN also has a separate `interceptions` group (picks CAUGHT) with the same key. The card's displayed rows stay unchanged.
- Refusals stay named: a player absent from a FINAL box is a named refusal, never a zero (books void a player who did not play); props on a non-full segment refuse; an unmapped player market refuses by name.
- Falsification test: a real ESPN summary (NE @ SEA, event 401872656) must grade to the box's own numbers; removing the passing-group scope must make a defender's pick change a QB's interceptions value; removing the prop branch must restore the refusal. RESULT: 6 of 6 mutations caught; end-to-end through `paper_settlement._our_verdict` on the real NE @ SEA box (Maye 178 passing yards grades LOST over 250.5 and WON under; Smith-Njigba anytime TD grades WON with the implicit 0.5 line).
- Files: NONE. Code landed (`2259edf8`, `0ceb9636`); its claims on `bet_status_nfl.py`, `bet_status_ncaaf.py` and their tests were HANDED OVER to lane `football-settlement-gaps` on 2026-09-10. The first-grade reading is TAKEN (2026-09-11 08:50 CT). Owed: the first NFL prop grade (scheduled task `nfl-prop-settled-grade`, 09-15).

Now `APP_LOG_MAX_PAGES = 400` (2.6x the largest hour on record) **plus a coverage check**: the pager walks backward, so if the oldest row held is more than 120 s after the window start the budget ran out and the poll reads UNDECIDABLE rather than dividing by a short total. **The switch's own evidence is unaffected — `capture()` uses 200 pages and the largest captured hour is 15,644 lines, so no capture in the set is truncated.** I verified that before trusting the 8.20-16.84 vs 0.35-3.05 separation.

### mlb-stop-publishing-edges — OPEN — opened 2026-09-08 — session d76b711b (adopted 2026-09-10 by df26ac0c; that session archived 2026-09-11, so UNOWNED) — **PRODUCT DECISION, USER-MADE, ON MEASURED EVIDENCE: stop publishing MLB live model-vs-market edges.** The model loses to the market over 252 games / 19 dates (pooled +0.00905, CI [+0.00154,+0.01686], excludes zero), the deficit is RESOLUTION not calibration (reliability gap CI spans zero; resolution 92-98% of the gap), and all three cheap fixes are closed by measurement: recalibration capped at 6.5%, no subpopulation survives leave-one-date-out, and the model/market blend is significantly WORSE out of sample.
- **GOAL VERDICT (reading 2026-09-12, session 50d991d1) — Goal (verbatim): "MLB live game-line rows are never marked `priceable`, are refused under a NAMED reason, and are STILL RECORDED in the ledger — publication stops, measurement does not — while WNBA, soccer and NCAAF are provably unaffected." → GOAL: NOT MET — 2 of 4 clauses proven on production.** **MLB: MET** (df26ac0c, 09-10 13:45 CDT, `deploys.md` 13:57 CT; re-read 09-12 on the per-record ledger: 1,230 records written, 0 priceable, 6 refused `model_edge_publishing_disabled_for_sport`). **SOCCER: MET 2026-09-12** — after the switch (recorded_at >= 09-10T18:44Z) 09-11 29 of 57 priceable and 09-12 276 of 928, 0 refused by the switch; a disabled sport reads 0 priceable by construction. Pre-switch rate 17.6-39.0% vs 29.7% on the one full post-switch day. **NCAAF: NOT MEASURABLE, and the null is not evidence** — 0 priceable on every date read, before (09-05, 09-06) and after (09-11, 09-12), all refused UPSTREAM of the switch; no NCAAF row has ever reached it. **WNBA: no ledger** (404 on 09-07/08/10/11), returns 09-17. **Left:** NCAAF needs a row that would be priceable, and none can be today (see the NCAAF trigger below); WNBA needs its return. Code-level isolation is the 14 mutation-checked unit tests. **USER DECISION 2026-09-12 ("wait for all"): do NOT close on soccer + the unit tests — the lane closes only when WNBA AND NCAAF are each proven on production.** **WNBA trigger:** its first post-break slate, 2026-09-17 (first tip 23:30Z); reading scheduled as local task `wnba-isolation-reading-0918` (2026-09-18 10:00 CDT), which records into this block. **NCAAF trigger: NOT SCHEDULED, because nothing due will change it.** Measured 2026-09-12 on the per-record ledger (2,268 rows): full-game spreads 740 and totals 682 refuse `live_resim_published_no_distribution_for_this_market` (the DELIBERATE one-market-family choice, `state_football.md`); full-game h2h 254 refuse `no_two_sided_market_price` although ALL 254 carry both `market_fair_prob` and `model_home_win_prob`, because `syndicate/features/ncaaf/game_projections.py` sets `market_fair_prob_over` only in its `totals` branch (L435/L482 on `origin/main`), not its `h2h` branch (L405); h1 rows refuse `segment_is_not_full_game`. So no NCAAF row can reach the switch until the h2h branch writes that field — and writing it could make NCAAF h2h rows PRICEABLE at the join, a publication change for a sport whose edge is separately withheld by a measured model gate (margin model loses to the close by 3.563 MAE, t=17.2). **That is a product decision, not a fix this lane can make; no OPEN lane owns it.** Owner: still UNOWNED (50d991d1 took the reading only). Table and method: `.syndicate/log/2026-09-12.md`. The superseded 09-11 verdict line is in `lanes_history.md` verbatim.

That lane's `- Files:` bullet reads *“No production code, no deploy, no ...”* naming the blueprint — a DISCLAIMER — but `lane_claims._claimable_prefix` cuts at the first marker in `_DISCLAIMER_MARKERS` and the bare negation `no ` is not one of them, so the prefix survives whole and the negated path reads as a claim. The 2026-09-03 comment in that marker list was added for exactly this failure with the word `never`; this is the same bug in a different phrasing. **So the lane most carefully avoiding the file is recorded as owning it, and the fix belongs in `.claude/hooks/lane_claims.py` — which is FORBIDDEN to verify from a worktree (`learnings.md` 2026-09-08: hooks run from the PRIMARY tree).** Not taken here; surfaced, not silently worked around. `bandwidth-controlled-transfer` was adopted ~20:0xZ today by session 1e77a7da and is actively firing arm 2, so its block is not to be edited by this lane.
- Hypothesis, WRITTEN BEFORE TESTING — **two ledger entries disagree about what a sync actually does, and the whole size of this problem turns on which is right.** `learnings.md` 2026-09-08 says a sync *“would DELETE 166 live env vars”*, counting the undeclared-but-live keys (33 + 77 + 56). `scripts/audit_blueprint_drift.py`'s own docstring says the opposite, from a measurement: the 2026-08-08 sync took refresh-worker **92 → 93** keys while the blueprint declared 84, and a full replace would have driven it DOWN to 84 — therefore **a sync UPSERTS declared keys and leaves live-only keys alone**, and the hazard is reversion, not deletion. The tool's live output labels all 174 of them `live-only (undeclared, upsert leaves these alone)`.   - **Why the approved P2 was dropped:** the overlap check plus whole-file-union fallback would misfire on nearly every cycle. refresh-worker appends its own rows between pulls, so every tail after a local append mismatches and forces a ~100 MB whole re-pull, the #241 restart-loop pattern. Its Layer 2 build reads those rows immediately (`layer2_shortlist.py:928` `read_book_quotes_latest`), so moving local writes to an outbox would delay venue prices on the board by a cycle.
  - **User decision: "Sync marker + tail rewrite (Recommended)".**
  - **The design:**
    - A sidecar `<shard>.webpos` records how many leading bytes of the local shard are web's bytes.
    - The tail pull requests `Range: bytes=<webpos - overlap>-` and verifies the overlap against local bytes `[webpos-overlap, webpos)`.
    - On a match, under a per-shard lock shared with `append_book_quotes`, it reads the local-only rows after `webpos`, truncates at `webpos`, appends web's new tail, re-appends the local-only valid JSON rows web's tail does not already contain, and advances `webpos`.
    - On a mismatch, or a missing or implausible `webpos`, it does ONE whole pull plus the local-only valid rows, and resets `webpos`. It is logged by name.
  - **Files amended:**
    - `odds_book_quotes.py` scope widens to the append lock in `append_book_quotes`.
    - RELEASED 2026-09-15 ~21:20Z to lane `soccer-live-scoreboard-range-stale` (see this block's Files line); the amendment as written: `artifact_publisher.py` stays `pull_streamed_artifact` only, plus a private helper.

    - it reads `.<shard>.webpos` (missing, <=0 or > size means the file size);
    - it asks `Range: bytes=<webpos-4096>-` and compares the overlap with our bytes;
    - on a match it calls `_apply_synced_tail` under the lock: cut at webpos, write web's new bytes, re-append local rows web's tail lacks (dropping non-JSON), advance webpos, log `STREAM_TAIL_SYNC_OK`;
    - on a mismatch, 416 or non-206 it calls `_resync_append_only_whole`: one full pull plus local valid rows web lacks, webpos = web size, log `STREAM_SYNC_WHOLE reason=`.
  - A missing local copy is a plain whole pull that now records webpos. The old blind `STREAM_TAIL_OK` append is gone.

    - **Switch.** `SYNDICATE_POLYMARKET_PRICE_AT_ASK`, ON only for `1/true/yes/on`. Absent means off, reproducing today's build byte-for-byte (the step-1 instrument unchanged). Nothing changes on production until the env is set AND a deploy carries it, and both are the user's call.
    - **Where.** `polymarket_us_submitter.build`, after `order_body` validates (the same place as step 1). No other file changes behaviour. `execute_portfolio`, `portfolio_commit` and `check_order` are untouched.
    - **Book.** One signed book read. If the ask for our side is unreadable (read failure, empty side), it REFUSES `ask_unreadable`. Unknown must not default permissive; this reverses step 1's never-block rule on purpose, and only when the switch is on.
    - **EV at the ask, net of fees.**
      - `p_model = planned_p * (1 + ev_pct/100)`, the same fair step 1 logs.
      - `cost = ask + fee_per_contract`, with `fee_per_contract = POLYMARKET_ASSUMED_WORST_CASE_RATE` (0.02, `venue_fees.py:465`). That is a bound above the measured 0.015 at mid-price and the 0.0107 measured at 0.235, stated as a bound.
      - `ev_net_pct = (p_model / cost - 1) * 100`.
      - It refuses `ask_ev_below_min` when `ev_net_pct < resolve_settings().min_ev_pct` (the plan's own minimum, default 2.0).
    - **Kelly at the ask.**
      - `k(c) = (p_model - c) / (1 - c)`.
      - `stake_at_ask = planned_stake * min(1, k(cost) / k(planned_p))`, so it only ever shrinks. `check_order` already charged the planned stake, so no cap can be exceeded.
    - **Price and size.**
      - The price sent is the ask, snapped up to the tick.
      - `quantity = min(floor(stake_at_ask / ask), floor(ask_qty))` to the market's increment.
      - Below one increment it refuses `ask_size_below_minimum`.
    - **Log.** One `POLYMARKET_PRICED_AT_ASK` line per build: planned price and stake, ask and size, fee bound, `ev_net_pct`, Kelly ratio, the stake and quantity sent, and the decision.
    - **Falsification.**
      - (a) With the switch off, a build is byte-identical to today's.
      - (b) With it on, a 20%-planned build whose ask sits 34 ticks above the quote (the measured `aec-nfl-car-atl` shape, planned 17.7, `ev_at_ask` 1.39) must REFUSE.
      - (c) A small-edge build at the quote must place at the ask with its stake at or below plan.
      - (d) A thin ask must cap quantity at `ask_qty`.
      - (e) A read failure must refuse.
      - Any of these failing means step 2 is not what it claims.
    - **Verification after an enabled deploy:** `POLYMARKET_PRICED_AT_ASK` lines on live-odds-worker, refusals by reason, and every placed order's sent price equal to the logged ask. Then fills vs refusals against step 1's population.

  - The build runs in-process in the intelligence loop thread (`pipeline/intelligence_state.py:7635`). Its return value feeds `maybe_record_board_state_to_evaluation_ledger` and `write_latest_intelligence_state` in the parent (`:7642`, `:7645`).
- Risks known up front: a subprocess per build is periodic work, and `#241` put this worker into a restart loop that way (`overview_subprocess.py` docstring). The child also needs the build's peak (+296 to +1,416 MB) plus interpreter and import cost inside a 4,096 MB container shared with the MLB sim.
- Hypothesis (to test before code): what the parent holds after a build is state built during it (module caches, loaded frames), which a child's exit would release. Falsified if a parent that has never run a full build still settles above ~2 GB once its other loops (fast path, venue loop, book grid) have run for 60 min.
- Verification: refusal and recycle counts per hour across a full slate day on the isolated build, against this lane's baseline (restart-at-1 period), plus parent anon after build and the child's peak and elapsed time.

- **DESIGN SURVEY 2026-09-14 ~15:35Z** (subagent, then re-read by this session; file:line below is re-derived):
  - The build's return value is plain JSON-shaped (`_build_candidate_pool` already round-trips through `json.dumps(..., default=str)`, `intelligence_state.py:7093`). Its parent consumers are pure functions over that dict (`:7642`, `:7645`). A file-based child handoff like `overview_subprocess.py` is feasible.
  - Must stay in the parent: `self._execution_guard` (`:7623`); `note_heavy_build_guard` and the memory guard (a child would measure its own memory and blind the recycle signal); the two in-memory build caches (they go cold per child).
  - Money-path side effects (`run_portfolio_commit`, `run_execution`) would run inside a killable child. Their idempotence must be confirmed first (see todo #657).
- **Hypothesis H-cache (written BEFORE any test, and cheaper than a child process):** the retained ~2.2 GB is mostly `self._candidate_pools`.
  - It is an OrderedDict keyed by (date, source_fingerprint) (`:5955`), filled at every successful build (`:7090`) and trimmed only by COUNT to `self._max_snapshots` = `max(5, SYNDICATE_INTELLIGENCE_MAX_SNAPSHOTS or 12)` (`:4779`, `:8501`).
  - The fingerprint changes as sources change, so the cache should fill to 12 full pools over ~12 builds and then hold. That fits the measured rise-then-plateau, and post-trim anon rising 1,989 -> 2,482 MB over 14:48-15:01Z.
  - The comment at `:7060-7064` records that this same cache was once "the dominant memory driver" when each pool embedded the sport odds-history payload. That embed was removed; the pool's current size is unmeasured.
  - `self._snapshots` is NOT the suspect: `STATE_PERSIST_TRIMMED ... of=4` means 4 entries, keyed by payload (`:394`, `:7757`).
  - **Falsified if** the cache's serialized total at 12 entries is well under ~200 MB. Python objects run several times larger than their JSON, so ~200 MB of JSON could plausibly be ~1 GB live; the multiplier is not measured here. Also falsified if the plateau is reached within the first 1-2 builds after boot.

### web-export-timeout — CLOSED 2026-09-16 — opened 2026-09-16 — session a1e40980-cceb-493f-adf9-5a5ca879acf6 — **GOAL: MET**
- **VERDICT 2026-09-16 ~22:05Z (17:05 CDT), checkpoint, session a1e40980.** Goal (verbatim): "name the mechanism by which web's `/api/ops/artifacts/export?pattern=*<today>*` exceeds the pull client's 30 s timeout, with a production measurement that DISCRIMINATES between the hypotheses below rather than being consistent with all of them. A fix is in scope only once the mechanism is named." — **GOAL: MET.** The reading: `names_only=1` isolates the walk; seven samples of identical work ran 13.10-38.13 s, while full-body exports ran FASTER. That discriminates H2 (walk) from H3 (bytes) and confirms H4 (truncation). **Every fix taken beyond the goal is shipped and verified:** per-file cap (all three), 48 MB budget, watermark resume cursor + 1 ms tolerance (6/6 on production), and the **90 s board-pull timeout: 0 of 12 dated pulls timed out vs 13 of 28, same-window 30 s control 12%; tomorrow's floor recorded by its own pull** (`deploys.md` 20:58Z, 21:28Z). **Not done, recorded as leads, not lane work:** the walk's cost (H5 below, hypothesis only) and the 2 h window clamp (measured biting, `deploys.md` 20:47Z). Both are user decisions. **Unread and not owed by this lane:** memory at long uptime; the client resume branch has not fired in production (nothing truncates at 48 MB).
- Goal: name the mechanism by which web's `/api/ops/artifacts/export?pattern=*<today>*` exceeds the pull client's 30 s timeout, with a production measurement that DISCRIMINATES between the hypotheses below rather than being consistent with all of them. A fix is in scope only once the mechanism is named.
- Files: **RELEASED 2026-09-16 ~22:05Z at lane close, everything shipped and verified, no claim held — re-claim before editing:** `syndicate/blueprints/ops.py` (the artifact-export PATTERN branch; this lane had taken it from `book-quotes-splice-repair`, which may re-claim it), `tests/test_artifact_export_oversize.py`, `tests/test_pull_watermark_truncation.py`, `syndicate/features/shared/live_lens_loop.py` (the one pull call's explicit 30 s), `tests/test_pull_timeout_scope.py`. `syndicate/features/shared/artifact_publisher.py` was released earlier to `nhl-season-readiness`. The full transfer history moved verbatim to `lanes_history.md`.
- **What is already measured** `[2026-09-15, lane soccer-live-scoreboard-range-stale]`: 10 of 60 `pattern=*2026-09-15*` request lines timed out (08:57-20:41Z, ~1 in 6), and BOTH workers failed within the same seconds at 20:17Z and 20:41Z. One miss leaves every today-dated artifact on refresh-worker stale until its next today-pull (~15-30 min), not only soccer. Web's access log carries no duration, so server time has never been read.
- Hypothesis (written BEFORE testing, four, and they are not all compatible):
  - **H1 CONTENTION.** The cost is CPU-bound — up to 24 MB of file bodies read and `jsonify`-serialized — and `json.dumps` holds the GIL, so `GUNICORN_THREADS=4` does not buy concurrency for it. Two overlapping pulls (live-odds-worker every ~2-3 min; refresh-worker every ~15-30 min) collide and each crosses 30 s. The same-second failures on both workers are what suggested this.
  - **H2 WALK.** `iter_pattern_matches` walks the tree for every matching pattern BEFORE `since` is applied (`since` filters after `stat`), so even a tiny incremental pull pays a full walk whose cost varies with page-cache state.
  - **H3 BYTES.** Duration tracks returned size, and the constant movers (mlb `book_grid` 9.38 MB, soccer 3.99 MB) dominate it.
  - **H4 BUDGET TRUNCATION** — a correctness lead, not a timeout cause. The 24 MB budget `break`s mid-walk, so a caller can silently receive a TRUNCATED set with no error. Consistent with the unexplained 2026-09-15 21:00:52Z sizing, where a 150 s window returned 21 files while 30 min and 2 h windows returned 7-8 at nearly the same instant — a wider window returning FEWER files is impossible unless something truncates.
- Falsification test: a SOLO, uncontended request at a representative `since`, repeated, that reproducibly exceeds 30 s falsifies H1 and moves the cause to H2/H3. `names_only=1` (same walk, no bodies) running consistently fast while the full export is slow falsifies H2. Duration not correlating with returned bytes falsifies H3. `truncated: false` on every sampled response falsifies H4.
- Verification: a latency distribution read from production — solo vs overlapping, `names_only` vs full, across `since` windows — that names ONE mechanism and says which hypotheses it rules out, recorded in `deploys.md` with the client-side failure rate re-read from Render logs. Measure before changing anything; `learnings.md` forbids optimising on a guess.
- **RESULT 2026-09-16 03:40Z — MECHANISM NAMED. H2 CONFIRMED, H3 FALSIFIED, H4 CONFIRMED and worse than the timeout, H1 unattributed.** Full evidence: `.syndicate/findings_2026-09-16_web_export_walk_and_truncation.md`.
  - The cost is the WALK, not the payload. `names_only=1` (same walk, same `since`, 16 KB of wire) on the production-shaped query, 7 samples of IDENTICAL work (129 files): 13.10 / 13.74 / 15.01 / 15.21 / 27.58 / 29.71 / 38.13 s — median 15.21 s, **1 of 7 over the 30 s client timeout**, against production's 10 of 60. The distribution straddles the timeout; nothing has to go wrong.
  - H3 FALSIFIED: full-body exports were FASTER (14.23 s, 3.16 s) than the walks, because hitting the 24 MB budget `break`s out of the walk. Bytes shorten the request.
  - That `break` also explains the 2026-09-15 anomaly the ledger left open: a wider `since` window matches the big movers sooner, truncates earlier in walk order, and returns FEWER files FASTER. Not a superset, so 21 vs 7-8 was never impossible.
  - **H4, the one that matters:** both full exports returned `truncated: true` (4 files for a 30-min window). `truncated` appears NOWHERE in `artifact_publisher.py`, so a truncated 200 reads as success, `all_succeeded` stays True, and the watermark advances past every undelivered file. They are then below the floor and never re-requested; the repair pass only fetches files missing OUTRIGHT, not stale ones. Same shape as `082da3e3`, different place.
  - The two modes are opposites: a pull with LITTLE to fetch walks the whole tree and times out (visible); one with MUCH to fetch truncates, returns fast, logs `PULL_OK`, and silently drops the rest (invisible).
  - **NOT established:** H1. The 15 s median needs no contention to explain, but the 13 -> 38 s spread on identical work is unattributed (page cache, disk latency, CPU steal, concurrent pulls all live). Do not report the variance as contention.
  - **No fix taken.** P0 is client-side (`truncated` must block the watermark) in `artifact_publisher.py`, and P1 is either a cheaper walk or a timeout above the tail in `ops.py` — both held by `book-quotes-splice-repair`. They interact: fixing the timeout alone makes truncation MORE frequent.
- **FIX LANDED `a347c4ea` AND LIVE ON WEB `1ddb5854` 04:04:57Z — verify PARTIALLY MET (`deploys.md` 04:01:38Z): `count` 4 -> 127 and `oversize_skipped` 5 with the accumulators NAMED (280.4 MB shed visibly), but `truncated` is STILL True against a predicted False, so files are still silently dropped — the binding constraint moved from the five accumulators to the 24 MB budget itself (~32 MB of capped-eligible files remain). The workers are NOT deployed, so the `PULL_INCOMPLETE` logging is unread in production.** Per-file cap (8 MB, env `SYNDICATE_ARTIFACT_EXPORT_MAX_FILE_BYTES`) applied BEFORE the budget test, so an oversize file consumes no budget and does not trip `truncated`; capped files are NAMED in `oversize` for `?path=`/stream. Client side is LOGGING ONLY (`PULL_OK` carries `truncated`/`oversize_skipped`, plus a `PULL_INCOMPLETE` line) -- watermark semantics deliberately unchanged. 6 tests, 5 failing on pre-change code; the 6th is a must-not-change guard (`names_only` still lists capped files). 243 passed across the publisher/walk/export/compression suites.
  - **It also closes a memory hazard:** the budget test is `total_bytes + size > budget AND artifacts`, so the FIRST file was admitted at any size — a walk reaching the 126 MB accumulator first would `read_text`+`jsonify` it on the 2 GB web instance. `test_an_oversize_file_is_skipped_even_when_it_is_the_ONLY_file` fails on pre-change code, so the hazard was real and is now pinned.
  - **DEPLOYED AND VERIFIED on all three: web `1ddb5854` 04:04:57Z (partially met), live-odds-worker 04:16:44Z (MET), refresh-worker `5abc20f3` 05:06:56Z (MET).** refresh-worker's first pattern pulls: `received=95 written=95 truncated=True oversize_skipped=5 oversize_bytes=238287619` and `received=77 written=77 truncated=True oversize_skipped=0`. Web's pre-fix probe of the same window returned **4** files.
  - **BUDGET RAISED 24 -> 48 MB, LIVE on web `62694269` 14:09:41Z, verify MET on a DISCRIMINATING read** (`deploys.md` 14:06:09Z): one `since` for inventory and export, 38.54 MB eligible across 134 files, and the export returned `truncated=False`, 134 files, 38.54 MB — a body the old budget could not produce. The first post-deploy read was NOT counted: 21.6 MB, under the old budget too. **Open:** memory impact is unmeasured (RSS 444/441 MB post-boot vs 630/551 MB at 10 h up before the deploy); the first post-boot request took 31.57 s, over the 30 s timeout; refresh-worker's own `PULL_OK truncated=` under the new budget is unread.
  - **Superseded by the budget raise above — RESIDUAL, and it reframes the remaining work:** the `*2026-09-16*` pull truncated at 77 files with **zero** oversize skips — so the budget binds on ordinary artifacts by itself, not just via the accumulators. Files are still silently skipped and the watermark still advances past them. The next step is a BUDGET decision (~32 MB eligible against a 24 MB ceiling, ~3x that in transient memory on a 2 GB instance), not another cap. NOT taken.
  - **Superseded: OWED: deploy + verify.** The cap is SERVER-side, so web is the deploy that delivers it; the workers gain only logging. Verification: after a web deploy, a `pattern=*<today>*` export returns `oversize_skipped > 0` with the accumulators NAMED, `count` in the ~100s rather than single digits, and the workers log `PULL_INCOMPLETE`. Until that is read, this fix is believed, not verified.
- **90 s board-pull timeout — `6ac08075` LIVE on refresh-worker as OFF-MAIN `793b621b` (= live D3 `b59887db` + `git cherry-pick -x 6ac08075`), `dep-dalere3m8hqs739b5jl0`, live 2026-09-16 19:33:20Z** (`deploys.md` 19:27:52Z). User decision: ship it ALONE ("Off-main, my change only"). Branch `deploy/refresh-worker-pull-timeout-90s-on-b59887db`. `5bd5c099` (on `9c98fd8f`) is RETIRED: it would have reverted D3.
  - **Composition:** reverts nothing (parent = live `b59887db`). Main's tip still carries it; the agreed queue is session 0f5b256e's #44 main-tip deploy (spacing-clear from 19:58:20Z), then `portfolio-no-family-exclusion`'s D2 redo.
  - **READING 2026-09-16 20:58Z: timeout half MET** (0 of 12; control live-odds-worker at 30 s: 12% same window; `deploys.md` 20:58Z). **Tomorrow-floor half MET 21:28Z:** the next `*2026-09-17*` request (21:18:52Z, OK) carried `since=1789590920.878161`, the prior pull's own start, with no clamp line (`deploys.md` 21:28Z). Original wording: dated `PULL_FAILED timed out` <= 15% over the first 12 dated pulls (baseline 13 of 28 = 46%, 16:38-19:12Z), and tomorrow's floor advances past 17:48:19Z. Counted across the main-tip deploy only if that commit contains `6ac08075`, split by serving commit. Watcher: scratchpad `verify_timeout90.py`.
- **H5 WALK BREADTH — hypothesis, written BEFORE any production test (2026-09-16 ~18:25Z).** The 90 s timeout treats the symptom; the walk is the cost. (a) A date subset does not narrow it: `patterns_that_can_match(HOT + EXPORT_ONLY, "*2026-09-17*")` keeps 165 of 184 patterns / 86 of 98 parents, because fnmatch `*` crosses `/`. (b) `iter_pattern_matches` runs `is_file()` (a stat) on EVERY basename-glob hit across all history, and `ops.py` applies the date subset only AFTER that. Local mirror (thin, lossy, NOT production evidence): 7,962 entries listed, 4,581 stats, 0-1 pass the date subset. Predicts: walk cost scales with history file count, not with what the pull returns; applying the subset before the stat collapses it. **Unexplained, and separate:** the 2-4x afternoon slowdown on identical work (candidate, untested: page/dentry cache eviction as web's memory grows). Not tested on production; a probe holds a web thread ~60 s, so keep samples few. A fix would touch `ops.py` / `artifact_walk.py` and is a separate decision.
- Blocked by: none. **Bandwidth discipline:** a full export is 15-25 MB per call, and this repo has an open lane measuring egress. Prefer `names_only=1`, keep full-body samples few, and state the bytes spent.

### soccer-postponed-served-final — OPEN — opened 2026-09-16 — session 452af042-6f2b-409a-ae87-432676aed12e
- Goal: A soccer match ESPN reports as postponed/canceled/abandoned (state post, completed false) is never served as FINAL or graded as a result: the chip for such a match is not state final, it is absent from the live aggregate finals, and a unit test pins it at the ESPN parse, the finals collector and the card state.
- Files: `syndicate/features/soccer/ingestion/espn_lineups.py` (`fetch_events` status mapping + a new unplayed-status helper), `scripts/poll_soccer_live_state.py` (the finals collector over `match_box` ONLY), `syndicate/features/soccer/cards.py` (`_effective_state_with_box` ONLY), `tests/test_soccer_unplayed_status.py` (NEW). **SCOPED next to OPEN lane `soccer-live-scoreboard-range-stale`** (user decision 2026-09-16, "open it with scoped claims and go ahead"): that lane holds the poller's two ESPN scoreboard window call sites and cards' `_live_state_entry`/`_live_vintage`; no function is shared.
- Hypothesis: ESPN serves a postponed match as `status.type.state: "post"`, `completed: false`, `name: STATUS_POSTPONED`, and `fetch_events` copies `state` verbatim into `status_state`, so every `== "post"` reader (poller `match_box.final`, aggregate `finals`, card/chip state) takes it as a finished 0-0. Measured: ATH @ LEV `401882870` 2026-09-16, ESPN type `{"name":"STATUS_POSTPONED","state":"post","completed":false}`, chip `state: final`, `0-0` on 3 reads (`deploys.md` 20:19Z).
- Falsification test: after the fix, a card or chip built from a record carrying ESPN's postponed status still reads `final`, or the finals collector still emits it -- then a second path sets final that does not go through `status_state`.
- Verification: offline, tests that fail on the pre-change code at each of the three points (parse, finals collector, card state) and pass after. Production, after a deploy: `/api/board/game-chips?sports=soccer` does not serve `401882870` as `final`.
- Blocked by: none. Deploy is a separate decision.
- **BUILT AND LANDED 2026-09-16 ~21:0xZ** (commit "soccer: a postponed/canceled/abandoned match is never FINAL or a graded result"): `fetch_events` maps `post` + `completed: false` (or a POSTPONED/CANCELED/ABANDONED name) to `status_state: "void"`; `_finished_matches` and `_effective_state_with_box` refuse an unplayed record, including a box written before the change (detail "Postponed"). Offline verification MET: `tests/test_soccer_unplayed_status.py` 9 tests, 6 FAIL on the pre-change code on their assertions (void constant, parse x3, finals, card state) and all 9 pass after. Neighbour suites: 124 passed; the 2 failures (`test_directory_hit_still_returns_the_real_tricode`, `test_real_roster_data_loads_silently`) fail identically on the pre-change code (need `data/`, absent from the worktree). Also touched in `cards.py`: two import lines. Side effect by design: a `void` fixture drops out of every `statuses={...}` caller (poller, `build_soccer_artifacts._fetch_fixtures`, completed-event stats), so it is neither boxed, priced nor graded. **PRODUCTION, 2026-09-16:** live-odds-worker `bf1785bf` live 21:43:25Z -- MET: the first la_liga live_state file after go-live (21:44:58Z) dropped the `401882870` box; the 3 played matches kept theirs (`deploys.md` 21:40:22Z). **Chip still `0-0 FINAL` through 22:20Z** -- held by `recommendations_2026-09-16.json` built 21:37:12Z by the OLD code (`status_state: post`), not by refresh-worker. Next soccer pregame autorun on live-odds-worker (4 h, ~01:37Z, same Central date) rebuilds it on the new code, which drops the void match. **OWED: artifact + chip reading after that rebuild; lane closes on it.** refresh-worker NOT deployed (HOLD on an in-flight MLB daily sim; the reading showed it would not fix the chip); `78932794`'s card guard reaches refresh-worker on its next ordinary deploy.

## 2026-08-17 - THE LEDGER IS A RECORD, NOT EVIDENCE (the inverse of the same day's other lesson)

I relayed *"two uncommitted soccer fixes at risk of being lost"* to the
coordinator as an action item. **It came from a lane entry, not from a
measurement.** `git status` was empty and fix #1 was already on main.

**This is the exact inverse of the three errors recorded above it today.** There
I called healthy things BROKEN from a null lookup. Here I called a committed
thing AT RISK from a written claim I never checked. **Same root cause: treating
a statement as a reading.**

`.syndicate/**` records what was true WHEN WRITTEN. This lane was last touched
two days before I quoted it. **Before acting on or forwarding a ledger claim
about the state of the working tree - uncommitted work, missing files, a broken
service - re-measure it.** The cost here was small (a wrong action item, since
retracted). The cost of the reverse - deleting or "rescuing" files on a stale
claim - would not have been.
